- **Session links in published history (F49)** (work item: 'WK-671', phase: '2') — **accept, with instrument** — the maintainer's ruling, 2026-08-30. The 73 stay in history. Removing them means rewriting a public `main`, which breaks every clone and invalidates every SHA cited across this register, the plans and the closure records — a cost out of proportion to an inert internal handle in a commit message. **The instrument is a squash-time strip**, and it is proven rather than intended: `git log <sha> -1 --format=%B &#124; grep -v 'Claude-Session:' > body.txt` then `gh pr merge <N> --squash --body-file body.txt`, verified on `98eca40` and `c8d3c81`, both of which reached `main` carrying zero links from branches that carried three between them. The merge is the only point where one person controls what lands, so it is the right place for the filter. **The prohibition is landed in `docs/process/delivery-process.md` §15 and `.claude/skills/git-hygiene`** in the same change, because a rule that lives only in a session dies with it — this one did, for the length of a day, which is why a member spawned before it emitted the trailer while the rule was notionally in force. **2026-09-02 — the runtime attribution guidance now emits the `Claude-Session:` trailer unconditionally and contradicts this ruling; F49 stands and the guidance amends nothing.** A runtime default is not a dated decision: the maintainer confirmed the ban that day, `delivery-process.md` §15 already carries it, and the product footer (`🤖 Generated with Claude Code`) is unaffected and stays. Recorded here so the next agent that meets the guidance finds the ruling instead of asking. **Two corrections to this row's own measurement, both found while wiring the check.** **(1) The predicate stated above does not reproduce the figure stated above, and did not when it was written.** At `32fc63c` the bare-URL form returns **77**, not 73; the trailer form (`--grep='Claude-Session'`) returns **73**, exactly the recorded figure. The four in the difference are `2e4684b`, which merely *quotes* this predicate in its body, and `ba76b3f`, `cbb8ffb`, `d2300f0` — real bare-URL violations, but all dated **2026-08-22**, eight days before this ruling and therefore inside the population 73 was meant to count. So 73 was a **trailer** count reported under a **URL** predicate. The figure is right about the trailer and the predicate is right about nothing; `RFC-777`'s rule that a count carries the predicate it counted with is what this row fails. **(2) The instrument did leak, but by 2 and not by 4**: `b2fb122` (#530) and `fbf483e` (#527) carry the trailer and are dated 2026-08-31, after acceptance. §15's *"enforced at the merge, which is the only point where one person controls what lands"* is true about control and false about reliability, and 2 is enough to make that point without inflating it. Enforcement moved to CI in the same change as this annotation — `.github/workflows/history-policy.yml`, deliberately not path-filtered, scanning commit messages only

- **`TraceStep.consumed`/`.produced` carry the full accumulated context, not the step's own declared inputs/outputs (F55)** (work item: 'WK-671 Slice 4 Task 4D', phase: '2') — carry forward — unowned. The remedy — trimming `TraceStep.consumed`/`.produced` to each step's own declared `consumes`/`produces` rather than the engine's full per-node context, or another slimmer trace-step shape — is a design change to `pricing_core.rating.score._build_trace` and to the `Trace` schema's semantics, out of Task 4D's scope (measurement, not remediation) and named by no workstream. It is also NFR-500's largest available remediation lever if the decision-maker chooses to bring the projection back under budget by shrinking trace size, rather than lowering the sample rate or raising the budget — a choice this row does not make. ***Cross-referenced 2026-08-31*** — F35 (NFR-490's latency finding) names the identical fix as its own remedy: trimming `TraceStep.consumed`/`.produced` to each step's own declared inputs/outputs. F35's remedy was gated on RL-862's off-path trace capture, which landed in WK-671 Task 4B (`003f9d4`, #485); that clears the precondition for building this shared lever but builds neither row's fix. Nobody had connected the two rows until this cross-reference.

Asks instead for a no-op cycle to be observable **without changing the state file's bytes** — two candidate mechanisms named without ranking (`os.utime()` on every cycle; a separate sidecar timestamp file), the choice left to whoever builds it. - **The lead-staleness nudge ladder cannot distinguish a legitimate long wait from a dead lead, and its disambiguating signal was withdrawn for cause and never replaced (F73)** (work item: '—', phase: '2') — **deferred with an owner — the lead**, 2026-09-27 (`CR-1167`, plan review 14; accepted by delegation 2026-09-27 16:18:24 BST). Event: plan review 14, the first act after the WK-697 close ([the maintainer (by delegation)], 2026-09-27 15:04:33 BST). This supersedes the owner and event of the opening kept below: carry forward, unowned — **not** a case for reversing F31 (the withdrawal remains correct; a false signal removed and not replaced is still better than one kept) and **not** a threshold fix (the ladder has no better information available, which is the finding). Per the shared-shape framing: recommend extending artifact B's model with a general, re-derived-each-cycle "work in flight" signal — Ruling-47(d)-compliant by the same construction as `position` and `in_flight_expensive_verifications` already are — rather than patch `nudge.py` alone or rehabilitate `roster-state.md`; that signal, once built, is what the reporter's charter already anticipates reading. Sizing, field shape and ownership not decided here. Falsifiable: discharged by such a signal landing and the reporter's charter updated to read it, or by a corrected reading showing the existing artifacts already disambiguate. **Amended 2026-09-02 — two further states, neither discharged by the "work in flight" signal above.** (1) **Work complete, waiting on an external decision** — the state this amendment is itself written in: zero open PRs, `main` at `81e7c4e`, `eta.md`'s headline says the team stood down with only the maintainer's decision outstanding; `nudge.log`'s own last entry (`07:15:02Z`, all three signals stale) is a live instance. "Work in flight" reads correctly `false` here too, so it cannot be the disambiguating signal; this state needs a "named outstanding external dependency" signal instead. (2) **A relaying agent's published field is checked; its internal narrative is not** — the reporter's cycle posts `eta.md`'s headline verbatim by design and that field stayed correct; separately, in relaying nudges at `06:15:03Z` and `07:15:02Z`, it narrated "W37-4 still building" — first reported by the lead as recipient, **then confirmed by the reporter's own quoted text, supplied on request after this row's first push**: "W37-4 still building (no merges expected, origin/main stale as expected)" at 06:15, "Expected pattern continuing during W37-4 build" at 07:15 — still an author's account of its own output, not this review reading the channel, but independently corroborated by this review against `nudge.log` on two details neither party was asked for (the "third"/"fourth" nudge labelling and both marker-age figures, both matching to within a minute). Independently verified false by 2h09m and 3h09m respectively (`#573` merged `04:06:14Z`; twelve further PRs merged before the second relay, confirmed via `gh pr list`). A third instance, per the lead: the reporter's own hedge narrowing this to "accurate through 04:15" is itself independently verified false, by nine minutes. The mechanism: `report-spec.md`'s re-derivation discipline covers structured facts (open PRs, merges), never the free-text narrative around them — nothing checks the belief the way something checks the field, and the checked field is the one that stayed right. Full reasoning: [`docs/findings/FD-01011-the-lead-staleness-nudge-ladder-cannot-distinguish-a-legitimate-long-wait-from-a-dead-lead-because-its-own-disambiguating-signal-was-withdrawn-for-cause-and-never-replaced.md`](../findings/FD-01011-the-lead-staleness-nudge-ladder-cannot-distinguish-a-legitimate-long-wait-from-a-dead-lead-because-its-own-disambiguating-signal-was-withdrawn-for-cause-and-never-replaced.md)

- **63 in-scope files cannot carry a header at all, and their custody is an exemption rather than a sidecar (F83)** (work item: 'W37-5c', phase: '2') — **accept, with instrument** — the maintainer's ruling, 2026-09-02. Exemption, **not** a sidecar; the sidecar is recorded as the option not taken so the design is recoverable if the 63 ever need machine-readable ownership. Two conditions, both enforceable and both owed by W37-5c: **(1)** every exempt entry cites its reason and the ruling permitting it — a list whose entries carry no justification is indistinguishable from a list of things nobody got round to; **(2)** the exempt set is itself checked, the count of unstamped in-scope files equalling the exempt list, **so the list cannot grow silently**. Condition 2 is RL-985's property applied to an exemption rather than a census and inherits its reasoning: the check **names** the unstamped-and-not-exempt files, never compares two totals, because two errors that cancel pass a total-only check. Filed as a finding rather than left as an allowlist so the 63 have custody under `RFC-778` — a deferred item with no owner is not deferred, it is lost. **Corrected 2026-09-02, after the ruling: the population is 65, not 63.** This row's claim and evidence cells both state 63 and are superseded here rather than edited, per this file's annotated-in-place rule. Two tracked files in the ruled stamp set were never counted, both meeting the finding's own criterion that a format with no comment syntax cannot carry front matter: `docs/process/delivery-process.core.json` (CLAUDE.md §15's machine-readable extract, where front matter breaks `json.load` for the reason the 59 contracts schemas do) and `docs/research/file-census-5ef559d.csv`. So 60 plus 2 non-`.md` files, plus the 3 unparseable vendored manifests, is 65; measured at `7186dca` and stable across `e63332c` and `544b90c`, since #629 touched no file under `docs/`. **The counting predicate is stated once, in F83's own dated correction section, and deliberately not restated here** — it contains a shell pipe that would split this row, and `CLAUDE.md` §13 refuses a pasted constant for the same reason `RFC-756` refuses a duplicated status line: the copy is what goes stale. **The defect was the corpus, not the arithmetic** — the lead measured the two populations already in mind and reported their union as the unstampable files, never enumerating the stamp set the rule ranges over, which is the class `F85` describes, committed by `F85`'s own author four hours after §13 was amended to require the predicate a count was counted with. The two new entries ship flagged in their own `ruling` cell as found by this check and awaiting ratification, reversible by deleting two tuples. **Condition 2 found them**, on its first day against the real corpus, which is the property it was imposed for. Full correction with the predicate: [`FD-01020-63-in-scope-files-cannot-carry-a-header-and-their-custody-is-an-exemption-rather-than-a-sidecar.md`](../findings/FD-01020-63-in-scope-files-cannot-carry-a-header-and-their-custody-is-an-exemption-rather-than-a-sidecar.md), section *Dated correction — 2026-09-02*.
- **Four counts stated as measurements that were never measured, in one day, by four different authors including a ruling and the lead (F85)** (work item: '—', phase: '2') — **accept as a practice finding; one amendment proposed.** `CLAUDE.md` §13 **already forbids all four** — *"a count carries the tree and the corpus it counted over"* — so this is not a gap in the standard but four violations of a standing rule in one day. **One element §13 does not require, and today made the case for**: the **predicate**. `2446` and `2519` were measured at the same tree over the same corpus, differing only by pattern, and **both satisfy §13 completely**; three readers reproduced neither and the lead published a false claim about a third party's work on the strength of it. Proposed to the maintainer, to accept or strike: *a count carries the tree, the corpus it counted over, and the predicate it counted with — the pattern, command or shipped constant, verbatim and runnable.* Discharged when §13 carries that clause **or** is dated as having declined it — **not** by the four figures being corrected, which they already are: a practice is not fixed by fixing its instances. **DISCHARGED 2026-09-02**: the maintainer accepted the clause, amended in one place — a shipped constant is cited **by symbol at that tree, never pasted**, because a pasted constant is `RFC-756`'s stale copy with extra steps. Landed dated in `CLAUDE.md` §13.

- **A foreground-blocked executor cannot receive a lead stop, and one relaunched its gate detached (FD-1214)** (work item: 'WK-1178', phase: '2') — **deferred with an owner — WK-1178**, 2026-09-28 ([the maintainer's (by delegation)] entries of 22:18:07 and 23:30:16 BST). #884 (`633c6f34`) delivered S-11 (foreground with a `timeout`), S-12 (never relaunch a killed process detached), S-13 (a full gate only on the lead's grant, under the slot flock) and S-14. **Two residues:** S-11 bounds a foreground call and does not make it receivable, so the lead's slot flock stays the control; and S-13 says a named single test needs no grant (`executor.md:109`–`:113`), so a targeted run needs no grant as written. The first quiet-window breach after the charter merged was executor-s1's **four** targeted pytest runs in `trees/executor-sanitiser` inside the lead's exclusive T7-2 window of 23:26 BST on 2026-09-28 (about 23:26:30 to 23:31:33, per executor-s1's account as the lead relayed it, reconstructed to within about 30 s; the last, PID 2301135, `949 passed`; commit `60f95f02` at 22:29:36Z, pushed at 22:31:43Z, read from git and GitHub). executor-s1 did not see the 23:26 notice during any of the four, because messages arrive only between an agent's turns, so the run broke the lead's window instruction and not S-13 as written; the lead let it finish because of S-14's residue risk. Fix, per [the maintainer's (by delegation)] entry of 23:33:55 BST: the lead writes a hold file (token and head); a root `conftest.py` `pytest_configure` hook refuses to run while it exists unless `GIP_GATE_TOKEN` matches, naming the holder; three tests (refuses without the token, runs with it, runs normally with no file) plus a positive control that the gate runner passes the token; S-13 amended to cover any pytest in a window and the frontend runner, or to state why not. Built by a WK-1178 executor after S3 merges, in the FD-1218 PR. Event: that PR merges and the auditor reads the hook, its tests and the amended S-13 in `executor.md`. **Second incident, 2026-09-29 (maintainer's entry (given on the maintainer's behalf) of 09:48:51 BST):** executor-m1 ran a full gate (PIDs 24822 and 26277, `trees/executor-m1c`, load 4.91) with no slot granted, inside S3's T7-3 window; the entry calls it a breach of S-13 and says the hook is still unbuilt. A stop is asked for in the entry and is not read here. **Third instance, 2026-09-29 (the maintainer's entry of 11:13:36 BST):** executor-s3fix started a gate without its merge-in and did not answer a STOP while blocked in the foreground; the maintainer accepted the running gate at `3a3e0277` as #886's full gate. The entry of 11:27:38 BST names PID 296584 (#886) as running at 10:27 UTC. No start time or STOP time is stated here. **As stated by the executor (executor-s3fix's own account, relayed by the lead; not read from a log here):** it launched #886's gate at 10:09:01Z on the lead's first grant, after the prior pytest (PID 224799) had exited at 10:08:29Z, and not from a wait loop; it ran the gate as a background job under `timeout 3600` and polled with `kill -0`, which deviates from S-11; and none of the lead's messages, including the 10:11:15Z STOP, reached it until the gate ended at 10:33:49Z.  **#891 closed unmerged, 2026-09-29:** the maintainer's entry "2026-09-29 11:35:28 BST · maintainer (acting on the maintainer's behalf) · #891: CLOSE unmerged; rework as a new WK-1178 PR" (`to-lead.md`) closes #891 and supersedes it with a new WK-1178 PR built to this row's Disposition. **The fix this Disposition specifies is now owned by that rework PR.** This finding stays open. **Fourth instance, 2026-09-29:** the maintainer's entry of 12:19:51 BST ("2026-09-29 12:19:51 BST · maintainer (acting on the maintainer's behalf) · LOAD incident: the #876 repro loops stopped by me", `to-lead.md`) records that at 11:19:28 UTC the 1-minute load was 20.15 and that four orphaned repro loops (bash PIDs 543605, 543606, 543608 and 543610, ppid 1) were each respawning `.venv/bin/python …/tmp/oldchild.py` at 135–240% CPU. They were owned by no live agent command. **They were stopped by the maintainer's kill of the four orphaned repro loops**, by PID and then their python children, at about 11:19:30 UTC; by 11:19:41 UTC none remained. The entry names it "the FD-1214 detached pattern again": a repro started by an agent that outlives the agent's command. The entry does not state how the loops were launched. **As stated by auditor-a-2 (its own account, relayed by the lead; not read from a log here):** the killed loops were its batch 3: `for k in 1 2 3 4; do ( for i in $(seq 1 30); do .venv/bin/python …/oldchild.py …; … done; echo done$k >> …/ofail.log ) & done; wait 2>/dev/null \| true; echo launched`. The `wait` was the last element of a pipeline, so it ran in a subshell and waited for nothing; the tool call returned at once, and the four `( … ) &` subshells were reparented to PID 1 when the shell exited. There was no trap, no PID file and no exit confirmation, and no `done<k>` marker was ever written, so batch 3 never finished. Batch 3 started at about 11:18:10 UTC, when batches 1 and 2 had completed, and its four subshells were still running their 30-run loops when they were killed at about 11:19:30 UTC; there was no respawning beyond the loops themselves. Batches 1 and 2 (completed earlier, 11:12:28–11:18:10 UTC) were `setsid nohup …/loop.sh <w> 40 >/dev/null 2>&1 &` four times: detached by design, no `nice`, no load or gate check. A later `pkill -f oldchild.py -u $USER` matched its own command line and killed its own call (exit 144), so it did not stop them. It withdrew all its reproduction counts as evidence. Ownership shape: event

Ruled: the cap is a typed setting defaulting to 64 MiB on both routes, and the nginx limit in `deploy/lb.conf` and in the new frontend configuration is the same limit plus a small margin.)* ***Resolved 2026-11-07*** — closed in place 2026-11-07 as the closing act of the mint of SL-2075 (WK-1178, LG-2076, PL-2074; ~~this PR; its number, squash commit and merge date are written at the merge's date-of-record~~ [PR #1456, squash 8194c04106ea8728e7cfe98c1b0c175c9779fd9f, merged 2026-11-07T21:19:03Z; filled 2026-11-09, docs batch 28]), in the FD-1842 form. Delivered remedy: one request-body limit on both upload routes, the typed setting `max_upload_body_mib` (`GIP_MAX_UPLOAD_BODY_MIB`, default 64 MiB), enforced by `BodyLimitMiddleware` (`backend/src/app/api/body_limit.py`) before any body is read when the length is declared and on passing the limit when it is not, answering the declared 413 `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` as an RFC 9457 problem with the request's `trace_id`; the proxy limit in `deploy/lb.conf` and `deploy/frontend.nginx.conf` is the same limit plus 1 MiB (`65m`), so the proxy never answers first. Red first, by cause, in LG-2076: `test_api_body_limit.py` red at `ebe2527a` (`3 failed, 2 passed`, `assert 200 == 413`), green at `d2490e18`; the proxy test red at `a029e461` (`deploy/lb.conf sets no client_max_body_size`), green at `0ecf4935`; A7's runtime limb passed at `8cf89fdc` (`1 passed in 132.19 s`: a 2 MiB body is not a 413, limit + 1 byte is the API's 413 `application/json`). What DP-A3 left out is not delivered here: XLSX decompression expansion and gzip's expand-first are bounded by neither limit, and are filed as FD-2031 (LOW, owner WK-1178). A pass (`LiveRefs.reconcile`, driven by `SwitchReconciler`) loads the latest committed Deployment of every Environment, never moving a ref backwards, and discovers Environments created after start; it runs every `Settings.deployment_reconcile_seconds` (DP-R1, `GIP_DEPLOYMENT_RECONCILE_SECONDS`, 1 to 30, default 10) and on every pub/sub reconnect (DP-R2, restart-equivalent hydration), and a failing pass is logged and the loop goes on. - The contract guard does not compare the objective-certificate check-name enum against the code's check vocabulary (FD-1349)
- The role files had no hard no-`cd` rule and no sweep-pause rule for a held gate slot, so three agents used `cd` despite their briefs and a read-only sweep ran beside a held gate (FD-1431)
- A custom metric whose `certificate_id` names no `metric_certificate` row passes the submission evidence check, because the check tests only that the column is not null (FD-1492)
- The approval withdraw route takes `artifact_is_live` from the request body, so the client asserts a fact the server must derive (FD-1510)

- The `pseudo_huber` template certifies `failed` on the platform default grid at delta 100 and above, because its loss `delta²(s − 1)` cancels catastrophically and the derivative check's noise floor does not model that (FD-1512)
- The `DagDesigner` live-validation debounce (400 ms, `useGraphValidation.ts:14`, `:34`) can fire an unstubbed `/rating-algorithms/validate` fetch inside a test, so the F39 guard (`test-setup.ts:22`) reds a loaded gate while the file passes alone (FD-1611)
- A default-live `POST /score` (no `rating_version_ref`) is refused with `INPUT_CONTRACT_VIOLATION`: `score.py:369-375` resolves the ref with `_serving_ref` but passes `score_one` the unchanged context, and the trace worker (`trace_handlers.py:98`) re-scores the stored ref-less context the same way (FD-1618)
- Attribution ignores sub-graph mounts: `_subset_algorithm` (`analysis.py:742`) carries the baseline's `sub_graphs`, `_subset_pins` (`:768-799`, over `_PIN_KINDS` `:484-489`) drops the baseline's sub-graph pins, and `_derive` (`:559-674`) never reads `diff.sub_graph_mounts`, so a mount's effect is silently attributed to an unrelated edit (FD-1660)
- FR-242's drafted change summary pairs rate-table pins by slug alone: `_pins_by_slug` (`rating_versions.py:1404-1408`, `{ref.slug: ref for ref in …rate_tables}`) keeps the last ref per slug, so with two pinned versions of one slug (valid under RL-1538) `draft_change_summary_for` (`:1377-1378`) silently drops one table's change or reports a false from/to pair, such as "X 1→2" while X@1 is still pinned (FD-1679)
- A failed API-key authentication writes its audit row and then raises inside the unit of work that discards it: `require_caller` wraps `authenticate_api_key` in `database.unit_of_work()` (`deps.py:240-242`), the failure audit is flushed (`service.py:85-110`, `audit.py:139-142`) and the raise rolls it back (`session.py:69-78`), so a revoked, expired, archived-account or environment-denied key leaves NO `audit_events` row (three HTTP runs at `8eaf6d5f`: 401 each, rows 0 → 0, table 576 → 576) and FR-392's "failed authentication … audited" is unmet; the only other record is one `4xx` tick of `gip_http_requests_total` (status is `status_code // 100`, `metrics.py:121`; no 401 series, no auth-failure counter), and a malformed or unknown key (no workspace) gets a WARNING log line only (`service.py:96`). `test_auth_keys.py:205-212` is green by construction: `pytest.raises` sits inside the `unit_of_work`, so the exception is swallowed and the transaction commits, unlike production. FR-392 cites `06` FR-368, which names no failed authentication (FD-1727)

Every id in the block appears in Tables A, B, C or `### Rows new at T` below with a resolution; Tables A, B and C were classed at `374a22bf33ad` and are not re-classed at T (their rows moved between A and B by T's markers; the join `scratch/plan-part3/g3join.py`, read 301 of 305 cells, and the four no-cell rows are named in the third part G3 "Join and its limit"). ## Findings

### The rule

`CLAUDE.md` §14: **"Nothing starts in the next phase while an open finding from the current phase lacks a resolution."** A finding has a resolution when the close fixes it, carries it forward with a named owner, or accepts it. The phase closure record lists every open finding with its resolution. G3 is that rule as an exit criterion.

### The counts and their predicates

Tree `374a22bf33ad3c62a4d2c8b69ba83bd334d4b167`; corpus `docs/findings/register.md`, every line beginning `|`, split on unescaped `|` into the five cells of `scripts/register-lint.py` (`Finding id, Concerns, Work item, Phase, Decision`), the header and separator rows dropped.

"Open" here means the predicate `scripts/register-owed.py` documents and `scripts/register-lint.py::_opens_with_status` implements: **the Decision cell does not open, emphasis stripped, with `resolved` or `fixed`** (case-insensitive). By that tool's own docstring this over-reports: a row carrying an appended, mid-cell `***Resolved …***` stays in it. Table B holds exactly those rows, so they can be checked one by one rather than assumed closed.

Predicate, verbatim (bash; `U` is the 0x1f separator):

```bash
U=$'\x1f'; P="^[^$U]*$U[^$U]*$U[^$U]*$U[^$U]*$U[^$U]*$U"
rows() { git show 374a22bf33ad:docs/findings/register.md | grep -n -E '^\|' \
  | sed -E ":a;s/^(([^|\\\\]|\\\\.)*)\|/\1$U/;ta" | sed -n '3,$p'; }
open() { rows | grep -v -i -E "$P[*_\` ]*(resolved|fixed)"; }                 # the opening predicate
open | grep -c -i -E "$P.*\*{1,3}(resolved|fixed)\b"                         # with a mid-cell marker, case-insensitive, as _STATUS_MARKER (Table B)
```

**Results at this tree.** Rows in the corpus: **383** (`rows | wc -l`). Open by the opening predicate: **311** (`open | wc -l`). Of those, with a mid-cell `***Resolved`/`Fixed` marker in the Decision cell (the `\*{1,3}(resolved|fixed)\b` test of `register_lint._STATUS_MARKER`, tested case-insensitively): **114**. Phase-cell distribution of the open rows (count, value): 294 2;9 1b;4 ~~2~~ 3;3 3;1 2/3/4.

**Table A (open; Phase cell trimmed equals `2` or `2/3/4`; no mid-cell marker): 181 rows.** Severity as extracted, Table A only: CRITICAL 0; HIGH 6; MEDIUM 34; LOW 40; not stated 101;  rows with no `owner` phrase: 26 (their Work-item cell is the only owner the row states, and `—` there means none).
**Table B (same phase test, with a mid-cell marker): 114 rows.** The register tool keeps them; each needs a reader's check (OPEN 10).
**Table C (open; Phase cell is not `2` or `2/3/4`, but it contains `2` (struck, moved to 3) or the row names a P2 Work by `WK-(668|669|670|671|672|673|674|675|690|693|694|695|696|697|1178|1250)([^0-9]|$)`): 7 rows.** Open rows outside A, B and C (phase `1b` or `3`, naming no P2 Work): 9.

**Extraction rules for the cells below** (by rule, not by reading each row): `id` is the last `(FD-n)` / `(Fn)` / `(F-W…)` token of the Finding-id cell, else the first `FD-` token in the row, else the register line; `severity` is the first whole-word `CRITICAL`/`HIGH`/`MEDIUM`/`LOW` in Concerns then Decision after text struck with `~~` is removed, then `Severity high|medium|low`, else "not stated"; `owner` is the first `owner …` phrase after the same strike, else `—`. These are extractions **as the register states them**, for the auditor to confirm: the first-match rule can read a severity or owner from a sentence about something else (OPEN 9).

**Resolution column:** the skeleton's "fix / carry with owner / accept" form. Each cell says how it was reached: read by this seat, "Appendix A; row not re-read", Appendix A's class, or left to the exit tree (OPEN 9, 10). Every one is a proposal.

### Retrospective (phase-close form)

The phase's exit criteria as predicates (`CR-1212` Proposal 1) let G1 to G6 be read mechanically and red-first, which caught G1 cells that a recollection-based count would have missed. Against that: every Work close that was delegated had to be re-affirmed by D-4 and D-5 at the end; NFR-489 was UNMET at exit and NFR-526, 527, 536 went unmeasured until their carry was ruled, because the spec host and the harnesses were not there when the requirements fell due; and WK-1178 absorbed findings as an exempt chain, so the re-owning map carried the debt rather than the phase discharging it. Reading for P3: measure on the spec host first (P3-A), and do not let a chain Work stand in for a finding's owner. *A proposal, the lead's.*

## Limits of this record

- **Rule-extracted table cells.** The Owner and Severity columns of Tables A to C are rule-extracted and can be wrong where the first match sits in a sentence about something else; the register, not this record, is authority (the earlier draft's OPEN 9).
- **Table B adjudication.** Whether a mid-cell `Resolved` fully discharges its row is read to the closer, not to its test, in the third-pass chain (`to-lead` 2026-11-09 12:14:13 GMT): "a passing assert proves the assert is still there, not that it proves the fix".
- **`CR-1212` comparison.** G4's list is `CR-1212`'s plus the S1–S3 readings (third part G4 (a)); `CR-1212`'s open-finding set was not compared row by row with Tables A to C.
- **Closed-in-prose rows.** The opening predicate and marker test see only `resolved`/`fixed`; §Dispositions 4 reads the 32 still-open rows; the 51 of the second pass are stated there.
- **§12 row 2.** Row 2 says "meeting NFR-489"; D-3 records it UNMET and accepts it as an interim for the P2 close, without addressing row 2's wording, which the third part marks as the user's word.
- **`gates:` field.** The roadmap's `## P2` `gates:` line still reads the pre-move dates; dated lines govern (`to-lead` 2026-11-09 14:58:57 GMT).
- **FD-2007 and FD-2034** are separate rows (`to-lead` 2026-11-08 08:50:28 GMT).
